# Hand-offs — work landed outside the ledger, waiting to be folded

A session that lands work it does **not** own `.dreamwork/tasks.md` for —
every session but the coordinator — appends ONE line under `## Pending`.
The coordinator reads it on every tick, folds each landing into the ledger,
and appends one `→ folded` line under `## Folded`. **Nothing ever moves**:
both sections grow only by append, so two sessions landing work at once
cannot lose each other's line — the property the dreamer inbox has and a
rewrite would not (#381).

**Section order (#406):** `## Folded` first, then `## Pending`, so an EOF
append lands under Pending and the instruction is true without a rewrite.

**Provenance notice (#469, 2026-07-29). `by grok` in a row below names the
HARNESS, not the model.** `ccc`'s `runner` field is the CLI harness: both
`@grok` (grok-4.5) and `@glm52` (glm-5.2) run the grok CLI, and the harness
exports only `CCC_PROVIDER` to the child — never the alias, never the model.
So **a lane cannot know what model it is**, and every self-reported model
name is a guess the harness's system prompt supplied. Two `@glm52` lanes
introduced themselves as *"grok-4.5 (xAI)"*.

Rows written before this notice therefore mean *the grok harness, model
unknown*, and they are deliberately **not** being back-filled: the dispatch
alias is not recoverable after the fact and a model attribution is history,
which is never guessed here. From now on **the dispatcher records the alias
it passed** — it owns that argument — and the model is derived from
`~/.config/ccc/config.toml`. Write `by ccc @glm52 (glm-5.2)`, never a name a
lane reported about itself.

One line per landing, mirroring the inbox's one-line-per-report shape.
Required: the task id (plain `#N`, sub-id `#Na`, or combined `#N/#M`), the
**sha** (what landed), who is claiming it, and a one-line `what`. The
`→ folded` line is how the writer marks one consumed, and it is itself an
append — never a deletion — so a folded hand-off is not flagged twice.

Only that path's EXISTENCE is consulted, never its contents, so a ledger that is really gone stays a loud ERROR in a main checkout AND from inside a worktree. The row WARNs rather than passing silently. Red-proofed BOTH directions plus two resolver guards; **a green red-run was found, reported and repaired** — deleting the `.git`-is-a-file guard left every test green because `read_text` on a directory raises `IsADirectoryError` into the same `except OSError`, so the test could not attribute the behaviour; it gained a `git init --separate-git-dir` case that only the worktrees-path check can refuse. CLIENT: buildChat renders a reply composer below the transcript (the .askform idiom), only for a found chat (the not-found degrade renders none). Reuses the EXISTING composer components, not a second surface: postJSON for the POST, DraftStore under a chat-specific key (chat:<id>, never the main composer's), the #255 confirmation lifecycle (confirmationFor) for success, Ctrl/Cmd+Enter submits. - **#508** · landed `5ad3dc0` · 2026-07-30 07:09 · by lane-508deploy (model per dispatch record — a lane cannot know its own model) — `just deploy` reported `deployed` against the OLD process. CONFIRMED MECHANISM (fixture, scratch_508 evidence): the deployed server ran `--dev` (=> autoreload); the recipe's `mv $snap.tmp $snap` overwrote the snapshot it watches, so autoreload `os.execv`'d the OLD pid IN PLACE (same pid, new generation, port flickered free via close-on-exec then rebound) — observed pid-unchanged/generation-changed; stop_deployed's 50ms poll can grade that flicker as 'free' and succeed while the old process rebinds; the new `nohup` server then dies on bind (EADDRINUSE, invisible); the curl-liveness readiness prints 'deployed' against the old pid (reproduced deterministically). STRUCTURAL root cause: success was LIVENESS (curl 200), not IDENTITY. FIX (true-by-construction): `verify_deployed` (dev/deploy_state.py) returns 0 only when `listening_pid==expect_pid` AND its argv runs the snap; `wait_port_free` confirms the port STAYS free (settle defeats the execv flicker) before the start; the recipe captures `$!` and calls `--verify-deployed --expect-pid`, echoing success only after it. `--dev` REMOVED from the deployed start line — autoreload was the re-exec enabler and serves no purpose on a frozen snapshot the recipe itself restarts (GENERATION still bumps on the fresh process). The PID is load-bearing over argv because autoreload's in-place re-exec keeps the old argv identical to the new snap. 9 new tests, all red-proved (named lines: success-return, pid-check branch ×2 via message+rc, _pid_alive, wait_port_free refuse, settle rebind-detection, --verify-deployed CLI branch, recipe --dev re-add). Identity check stands regardless of the --dev decision.

- **#567** · landed `ef112b1e` `bd990fe9` · 2026-07-31 · by lane-567deploy — the page-triggered deploy no longer self-bricks. `_default_deploy_runner` now spawns `just deploy` detached: own process group via start_new_session=True and output to a FILE deploy.log beside the deployed dir's serve.log, never the capture_output pipe whose read end the dying server held. The recipe's --stop-deployed kills the very server running the runner; the old runner's pipe closed with it, so the recipe's first print after the stop broke the pipe SIGPIPE and died mid-flight before shipping the snapshot or starting the new server — dashboard dark until a shell redeploy. The runner thread still wait()s so the single-flight slot holds for the deploy's life; when the server is stopped the thread and slot die with the process and the new server starts clear. Log-file choice: ~/.cache/dreamwork/deployed/deploy.log beside serve.log — NOT the target's .dreamwork/, because watched_mtime walks that tree and an append-mode log would bump the /mtime poll on every printed line arming spurious mid-deploy reloads. What releases the slot now: proc.wait returns on a clean recipe exit, or the whole process dies when the recipe stops it and the module-level slot dies with it — either way the next server starts clear; no timeout, by design, because one that outlives the spawner is the other half of the brick. Born-red is the INCIDENT: a standin parent server — a separate process importing the REAL watch — spawns the REAL _default_deploy_runner against a fixture recipe that prints, stops the parent, prints again; that print-after-stop is the write that broke the pipe. Born-red against the capture_output runner: marker.done never written, with the standin-really-died precondition passing BEFORE the failure — so it is the genuine mechanism, not a hollow red. Green against the detached spawn: new listener up and identity-verified with a pid different from the standin, marker written, and AFTER-STOP really in deploy.log. Red-proof on the named line stdout=log + start_new_session=True Popen: cp-backup watch.py, revert the body to subprocess.run capture_output, the test FAILs at marker.done, cp-restore byte-identical via cmp, no green red-run. Existing deploy tests unchanged and green: TestDeployAction 6, test_user_events_http 15, test_deploy_state 39; lint clean for my files. Lane-owns: watch.py deploy-runner region _default_deploy_runner / start_deploy / _handle_deploy docstring + the deploy-runner test only. NO FLAGs — the fix needed no justfile recipe change the runner is entirely watch.py; deploy.log is a human-read diagnostic not parsed by any tool so it needs no file-formats.md entry. Found-not-fixed: the fixture answers did-page-deploy-EVER-work — NO. Under the capture_output runner the recipe deterministically dies at the first print after the server is stopped, so page-deploy never completed end-to-end via the page trigger; every actual redeployment was a shell `just deploy` whose stdout is a terminal not a server-held pipe. The POST returned 202 and looked like success, which is why the brick read as a dark dashboard rather than an error. The fix makes page-deploy work for the first time. `git commit --only`; never just test; one free standin port outside 39880-39899; no attn, no pkill -f.

- **#592** · landed `f56cbba9` · 2026-07-31 · by lane-592lint — `lint.py` no longer reports a FALSE `ERROR tasks.md — no 'Next id'` inside a lane worktree, the red that #565/#569, #583 and #586 each passed on as “pre-existing, not mine”. Measured before accepting the diagnosis: unfixed `lint.py --target <worktree>` exits **1** on that ERROR; `--target <main checkout>` is clean in store mode. Root cause is narrower than “the ledger is gitignored”: `source_of_truth` reads the cutover watermark **out of `ledger.sqlite3` itself**, so an absent store answers `'markdown'` — indistinguishable from a repo that never cut over — and `check_tasks` falls to the #458 migration-notice shim, which has no `Next id` header. Chose the CLASSIFY fix over resolving the ledger from the main checkout, because reading another tree's ledger DATA would break the `--target` boundary; but classifying on “am I in a worktree?” alone would be a blanket silence, so new `lint.shared_store_for_worktree` resolves the main checkout from git's own record (a linked worktree's `.git` is a FILE holding `gitdir: <common>/worktrees/<name>`) and the excuse is spent only when ALL THREE hold — the store is genuinely absent here, this is genuinely a linked worktree, and the shared checkout genuinely carries the store. Only that path's EXISTENCE is consulted, never its contents, so lint's findings still describe nothing but its own `--target`, and a ledger that is really gone stays a loud ERROR in a main checkout AND from inside a worktree. The row WARNs rather than passing silently — the ledger checks did not run and saying so is the refusal to fake having run, the same shape as the store-mode watermark row in `check_review_decisions`. Red-proofed BOTH directions, `cp`-restored and `cmp` byte-identical after each: excuse disabled → the 2 worktree-clean tests go red; excuse unconditional → all 3 “still ERRORs” tests go red; store-present guard removed → its resolver test goes red; worktrees-shape guard removed → its resolver test goes red. **A GREEN red-run, reported not swallowed:** deleting the `.git`-is-a-file guard left every test green, because `read_text` on a directory raises `IsADirectoryError` into the `except OSError` path anyway — that test could not attribute the behaviour to the guard, so it gained a case that can: `git init --separate-git-dir` gives a MAIN checkout a `.git` FILE, the same shape a worktree has, and only the `<common>/worktrees/<name>` check refuses it. Fixtures are REAL git worktrees of REAL post-cutover repos with a REAL absent store (`lessons.md:336` — the trap is a test that asserts “no ERROR in a worktree” and passes because its fixture kept a ledger); for the same reason `test_this_repo_passes_its_own_linter` gained a precondition pinning store mode, since `frozen_tree` IS a linked worktree and a materialization that quietly failed would now leave the dogfood passing on this excuse rather than on real data. 8 new tests; 441 `test_lint.py` + 94 in the touched modules pass. `lint --target <worktree>` is now `clean (3 warning(s))`, exit 0; `--target <main checkout>` unchanged at `clean (8 warning(s))`. No doc paragraph was added (`lessons.md:405` in reverse — fix the classification, not a second description of it); the precedent WARN it mirrors is undocumented in `file-formats.md` too. Never `just test` / `just guards`; `git commit --only`; no `attn`. FLAG for coordinator: every lane brief still tells lanes to verify with `lint.py`, and this WARN now appears in every worktree run — it is honest, but if a lane's clean bar is “no WARNs” it will read as noise; the bar should be stated as “no ERRORs”. Found-not-fixed: `check_ledger_sections` / `check_task_origins` print NOTHING in a worktree rather than saying they examined nothing, which is the same house rule this WARN was written to obey — worth a task.

- **#598** · landed `f9621b7d` · 2026-07-31 · by lane-598notfound — an unrecognised path no longer serves the raw Python `http.server` 404 (visual audit D3, the one surface outside the design system). ENUMERATED FIRST, because a fix that beautifies one of several leaves the finding true: **seven** requests reach a `send_error(404)` — the `do_GET` fall-through, the `do_POST` fall-through, `/filedata`, `/chatdata`, `/reviewraw`, `/researchraw`, and `_send_bytes` for `/filebytes` — and all seven were measured serving the identical 455-byte stock body. Two of them (`/reviewraw`, `/researchraw`) 404 **inside** the review/research `<iframe>`s, so the defect had a second visible instance the audit did not reach. The fix is therefore at the `Handler.send_error` **seam**, not at a route: an allowlist of "the ones a human can see" rots the next time an endpoint is added, and one seam has no list. **404 only** — 421/403 are `_preflight` refusing a caller that is not the reader at all, and dressing a refusal in ~118 KB of his stylesheet is a ~260x amplification of the stock body spent on someone it is not for; every 5xx here is raised inside a `do_POST` handler so none is reachable by typing a URL (that scoping is itself asserted). **The status line is untouched** — this replaces the BODY of a 404, and a test proves it by failing when `send_response(200)` is substituted. SHAPE DECISION, against the audit's own suggestion: `routeOf` falls through to `{name:'dashboard'}`, so serving `page` for `/tasks` renders THE DASHBOARD under a 404 status line — confidently wrong, which is worse than ugly. Making the shell shape correct means a new client route name plus an entry in every per-route table it feeds (`TINT`, `TITLE_ROUTE`, `TITLES` — the machinery #302/#318's diff polices, and D4 is currently open against exactly that), and ~600 KB of JS booted to say one sentence on the path a scanner hits hardest. `client/router.js` is also held by another lane, so that option was not reachable here even had it won. DREAMWORK.md's *"two renderers only agree on the day they are written"* does not bite: `NOT_FOUND_PAGE` is `page_shell` — the one shell — around class names `client/style.css` already defines (`.qmissing`'s dim rail, #452); it declares no colour, font or spacing of its own, and a test strips `watch.STYLE` and asserts nothing styling-shaped remains. What it deliberately does NOT share with `buildChat(null)` is the COPY, because the copy has to differ — a wrong address is not a removed chat, which is the audit's own O2 about reusing that sentence for a second cause. Nothing from the request is reflected (no client-controlled bytes in the body, so the page is a constant built once at import with no escaping to get right); `target="_top"` on the way back because of the iframe sites. Red-proofed with the corrected recipe (snapshot the FIXED file, revert, watch, restore from THAT snapshot, `cmp` after every restore — never `git checkout`), five discriminating failures on named production lines: (1) `if code != 404:` → `if True:` — `/tasks` serves the stock markup again; (2) → `if False:` — the 421 refusal starts paying for the stylesheet; (3) `send_response(code)` → `send_response(200)` — `/tasks` returns 200 and the status assertion catches it; (4) `NOT_FOUND_PAGE` built without `page_shell` — the one-stylesheet assertion goes red; (5) **the fix this brief warns about** — style only the fall-through and leave the five data endpoints stock — fails naming `/filedata`, which is the evidence the enumeration is load-bearing rather than decorative. **Red 4 was also a finding, reported not swallowed:** `test_non_404_errors_keep_the_stock_body` bounded the 421 body's SIZE relative to `NOT_FOUND_PAGE`, so it went red when `NOT_FOUND_PAGE` shrank — a different defect wearing that test's name. Re-stated as `assertNotIn(watch.STYLE, body)`, which is the claim it was actually making. The stock-404 markers the tests assert ABSENT are first asserted PRESENT in `http.server.DEFAULT_ERROR_MESSAGE` rendered at runtime, so a CPython reword fails loudly instead of making them vacuously true. Verification: `pytest test_watch.py test_client_assets.py test_styleguide_audit.py` = 475 passed, 57 subtests (test_watch 442 → 445); `lint.py --target <lane worktree>` clean (3 known warnings: #592's worktree-ledger WARN, status.json ephemera, lessons near-dup). Rendered at 1440 and 390 with headless chromium off the constant: dark field, mono, dim rail, accent link, no sideways scroll at 390. **FLAGS for coordinator:** (1) `watch-design.md` has no section for the not-found surface and it is coordinator-owned — the page's rules (design system, honest status, no reflection, 404-only scope) live only in the `watch.py` docstring; worth a paragraph at merge or a follow-up doc task. No terminator at all is **30 of 99** live claimers, so not hypothetical: no guessing at an implied sentence, the whole field is the candidate and the cap bounds it with `…`. THREE sites, not one: `malformed` holds the same physical line and carried the same body, firing on no live entry that day, which is exactly why fixing only the loud branch would let it resurface. Live: longest quoted field 4568→200, median 1470→188. - **#595/#597** · landed `0332cc67` · 2026-07-31 · by lane-595scroll — visual-audit D1/D2/D5 (+O7 verified): the page stopped scrolling sideways at 390px and stopped snapping 5px on every route change. **START WITH THE GUARD WAS RIGHT, AND THE GUARD EXISTED.** `hfit.mjs` already asserted `scrollWidth <= clientWidth` on `/`, `/questions`, `/answers` at 390px — exactly the styleguide's promise — and PASSED while the live dashboard scrolled 28px. Its assertion was never wrong; its SUBJECTS were. #312 wrote a non-vacuity precondition for the `.cmdmenu`, the subject it was written for, and the two it acquired later are crumbs whose LENGTH comes from data (`target` = this checkout's absolute path, `version` = an arbitrary-length migration filename) against a fixture whose skill-version happened to be 18 chars vs the live 42. **The generalisable finding: a precondition belongs to each SUBJECT, not to the guard, and a check whose subject is a length needs that length asserted.** Second gap: `/file` was never in the route list at all, though the sentence promising no sideways scroll is in the FILE VIEW's own styleguide section — asserted about one pane, written about the page. **SECOND GUARD FINDING, and it is bigger than #597: Playwright passes `--hide-scrollbars` in headless by default, so EVERY guard in `dev/capture/` is blind to the entire class of scrollbar-driven layout effect.** #597 was structurally unseeable by the suite. New guard `gutter.mjs` is the one launched `ignoreDefaultArgs: ['--hide-scrollbars']` and its FIRST check refuses to grade until it has confirmed the scrollbar consumes width in the browser actually running, so a Playwright upgrade cannot quietly turn it back into a green light. **Fixes.** #595: `.wrapany` is now the one named wrap exception (`.fdir` was its first user, not “the ONE exception” as #284 said — that framing is what let three more unbounded values be written past it), carried on an INNER element at the `crumbsFor` build sites so the crumb keeps the `nowrap` that holds its own separator. `.mdfile` KEEPS its nowrap and `mdFileUnit` (components.js) splits the path instead: head in `.wrapany`, short tail bare, so the boundary before the #506 pip is unbreakable by construction. #597: `html { scrollbar-gutter:stable }`. **Measured, not assumed — three claims checked because the brief said to.** (a) The audit's “O7 fixed for free” is FALSE: `#dreambg` is `100vw` and `vw` counts the classic scrollbar whatever the gutter says, so the ~10px overhang is identical before and after; O7 stays open, correctly, as an opportunity. (b) No permanent gutter on touch — under Chromium's `OverlayScrollbar` the computed value is still `stable` and the 390px column is the full 358px vs 348px classic. (c) The price is 10px of reading column on classic scrollbars on the routes that did not scroll — constant instead of jumping. **LINE-BREAKING FINDING that changed the fix.** The obvious CSS-only #595 fix (put the wrap exception on `.mdfile > code`) was written, shipped into a red proof, and MEASURED WRONG: swept over path lengths 30..120 at 390px the pip orphaned at six of them. A break is allowed wherever the INNERMOST inline box ending at that position permits one, so `nowrap` on an ancestor does not save a pip that follows wrappable text — verified against four more CSS-only shapes (word joiner, nowrap `::after`, inline pip, inline-block unit), all six orphans, all of them. **TWO OF MY OWN CHECKS WENT GREEN ON THEIR OWN RED PROOFS — reported, then rewritten.** (1) `gutter`'s frame sampler ran a fixed 40 frames, ended ~660ms in before the departing route's dissolve let the page shrink, and reported ONE clean x value about a page that snapped a moment later; now samples until the flip is observed and has a named check asserting the window CONTAINED it. (2) `hfit`'s pip check tested one path length; the pip orphans at ~1 length in 14, so it passed the WRONG fix. Now a 30..160 sweep — that sweep is the only reason the correct fix shipped. **Red proofs**, each on the line its message names, `cp`-restored and `cmp` byte-identical, never `git checkout`: scrollbar-gutter removed → `#htitle.x` visits [436.2, 441.2], the audit's exact numbers; crumb `.wrapany` removed → dashboard only, `/file` untouched; `mdFileUnit` stops splitting → `/file` only, dashboard untouched; `.mdfile` nowrap removed → the orphan sweep only; fixture value shortened → the length precondition only. **Verification:** `hfit` (21 checks) + `gutter` (15) green solo through `just guards` on :39892; a 17-guard affected subset on :39891 all PASS (`qlinkpip filehead headcrumb mdquote mdtable headertravel reflow fileview filehl resize artifactwrap reviewsplit markrail qdual`) because `scrollbar-gutter` shifts layout 10px even under hidden scrollbars and that needed checking; `test_watch.py` 439 passed (one test updated — it bound `.fdir`'s rule BODY to the wrap properties; now asserts the element carries `.wrapany` AND `.wrapany` means wrap-anywhere, red-proved); `test_styleguide_audit/client_assets/question_dual_column/review_artifact/guard_evidence/guard_argv` 164 passed; the worktree's own `lint.py` clean. Never `just test`, never the full suite. **OUT-OF-LANE-OWNS, flagged for your call:** `justfile` (guard registration is mandatory — lint would WARN otherwise), `test_watch.py` (my refactor broke one test; leaving it red is worse), `transitions.md` (#597's governing doc — one paragraph: a change the page did NOT author is not a transition to be made graceful, it is a change to be prevented). `client/router.js` + `client/components.js` were cleared by you mid-lane. **Coordinator note:** branch base `27ecd4ad` is behind master `e443acae`, so `lint.py` from the main checkout reports 19 spurious `review/ … is stale` WARNs against this worktree — the same linter-resolution issue lane-600amber already filed; the worktree's own `lint.py` says `31 artifact(s), none stale`. No `attn`, no merge/push, :35110 untouched.

- **#620** · landed `49552469` · 2026-07-31 · by lane-598notfound — `watched_mtime` no longer watches sqlite's `-shm` sidecar, which was holding the 2s change gate permanently open (#614 plan, phase 0). MEASURED LIVE, READ-ONLY, before touching anything: 15 samples at 2s on the real tree with no writes by the measuring process — `watched_mtime` changed **15 of 15**, and `.dreamwork/ledger.sqlite3-shm` **held the maximum mtime in 14 of them**; with `-shm` excluded, **1 of 15**, and that one was a real `handoffs.md` edit. Re-confirmed against the shipped module afterwards: **10/10 as if unfixed, 0/10 fixed**. Over a separate 40s live window `-shm` moved on 20 of 20 samples while `-wal` moved 4 times and the db file 4 times — `-shm` was moving ~5x more often than the store was changing. The consequence is not cosmetic: the client refetches `/data.json` (917 KB uncompressed, ~200 ms of `collect()`) whenever the number moves, and serving that read touches `-shm`, which moves the number, which schedules the next refetch — the gate that exists to make the poll cheap was holding itself open, per open window, forever. **`-wal` IS DELIBERATELY NOT EXCLUDED, against the brief I was given** (which said "the `-shm` and `-wal` siblings"; the coordinator withdrew the `-wal` half mid-task and asked me to settle it by measurement). Measured both directions on a real store: a write moves `-wal` every time; with `-shm` alone excluded a write STILL advances `watched_mtime`; with `-wal` also excluded a write NO LONGER advances it at all. That failure is silent and in the dangerous direction — the dashboard simply stops noticing real change and nothing goes red. `-wal` is the write signal; `-shm` is the read's bookkeeping. **A SUFFIX RULE (`WATCHED_MTIME_IGNORED_SUFFIXES = ("-shm",)`), not the two filenames #614's plan listed** (`ledger.sqlite3-shm`, `user-events.sqlite3-shm`): the rule being expressed is "a sqlite shared-memory index is not content", which is true of every store, and `.dreamwork/` is already scheduled to gain a third (`session-index.sqlite3`, the session-log plan) — a name list would reintroduce this exact defect silently on the day that lands. It filters `kept`, so it leaves the #481 LISTING FINGERPRINT as well as the mtime max, which is a **second independent arm** of the same defect and not a bonus: sqlite creates and deletes the sidecars around a single read's open/close, and with no other process holding the store that churn moved the fingerprint on its own (measured 1 of 8 read-only polls before, 0 of 8 after). Two born-red tests; three discriminating red-proofs on named production lines, corrected recipe throughout (snapshot the FIXED file, revert, watch, restore from THAT snapshot, `cmp` byte-identical every time, never `git checkout`): (A) remove the `endswith` arm → both tests fail, on the `-shm` touch and on the listing fingerprint; (B) suffixes = `("-shm","-wal")` → *"-wal no longer moves watched_mtime"*; (C) the two-name list instead of the suffix → only the listing test fails, naming `session-index.sqlite3-shm`, which is the discrimination between the rule and an enumeration. **A FINDING FROM THE RED-PROOF, reported not swallowed:** red B initially failed on the test's PRECONDITION ("exactly one sidecar matches the excluded suffix"), so the `-wal` write-signal assertion — the one I claim matters most — was never reached and had never been shown to discriminate. The precondition now asserts only what it should (sqlite really writes both sidecar names) and carries no reference to the suffix tuple; red B re-run fails on the assertion itself. **TWO PROBES WERE VOID AND ARE NAMED SO THEIR NUMBERS ARE NOT REUSED:** my first two ran under `/tmp`, which is **tmpfs**, and tmpfs does not update mtime for mmap'd page writes — the `-shm` question is literally unanswerable there, and both probes reported "reads never move `-shm`". Re-run on btrfs beside the repo. **ALSO NOT REPRODUCED, stated plainly:** #614's specific claim that *one* `/data.json` GET moves `-shm` in isolation. On a quiescent copy a read moves it only when the store has had a write since the last read (WAL read-mark semantics), so a hermetic end-to-end "serve `/data.json` twice, assert no advance" test would be VACUOUS rather than red — I did not ship one. The live 15/15-vs-1/15 measurement establishes the defect directly, and the two shipped tests encode it deterministically (`utime` the sidecar, assert the gate; `utime` `-wal` and the db, assert it still fires). `status_derive.py`'s cache-invalidation docstring named `-shm` as part of the mechanism and is corrected in the same commit (single-source rule); its claim is unchanged and still true via `-wal`. Verification: `pytest test_watch.py test_status_derive.py test_client_assets.py test_styleguide_audit.py test_ledger_store.py` = 505 passed, 57 subtests; `lint.py --target <lane worktree>` clean. **FLAGS for coordinator:** (1) the `/data.json` **size and lack of gzip** are untouched and are the other half of the cost — this fix stops the *spurious* refetches, it does not make a real one cheap; #614 phases 1–2 own that. (2) `dreamhub.py` polls the same pair and gets this fix for free, but only if it is running the same `watch.py`. (3) I did NOT add `-journal` (rollback-mode sidecar): its existence is correlated with a real write, so it is a true if noisy signal — worth a look only if a store ever runs outside WAL. (4) Probe scripts left in the scratchpad under `nf598/probe620*.py` if anyone wants to re-measure. Never `just test` / `just guards`; `git commit --only`; no `attn`; :35110 untouched and no server bound to it.

- **#632** · landed `7da08cba` · 2026-07-31 · by lane-632qloss — **root cause `watch.py:715`: `read_text(path, limit=200_000)` truncates SILENTLY, and `/answer` did read→append→write through it** — questions.md was 230,876 chars, so the handler appended his answer to the first 200,000 and wrote that back over the whole file. **Not a retention cap and no archive was ever missing: the file was CUT.** "The 12 OLDEST entries" was the misdirection — `## Answered` is newest-first, so the oldest sit at the END of file; oldest-deleted and tail-truncated are one event seen from two directions and only one points at the cause. Three independent agreements: the damaged copy ends mid-word ("…Therefore the durable que"), which no policy produces; clean-room replay of the exact recovered POST body (`.dreamwork/submissions.log` 2026-07-31T17:03:02) against `base[:200_000]` reproduces the measured 200,366 chars to the character; and `## Open` GREW 407 while `## Answered` lost 30,875 — gain at the head with loss at the tail is a short READ, not a delete. `append_subbullet` is append-only and exonerated (replayed untruncated it returns 231,241 chars / 63 answered, losing nothing). **The stale-process hypothesis was REFUTED by measurement**: `--dev` implies `--autoreload` (`:5556`) and `os.execv` (`:5521`) preserves pid AND `/proc` starttime, so pid 3443196's "3 days" was an execv artefact — its autoreload thread tid=2502743 dates to 2026-07-31 17:16:22, exactly watch.py's mtime, and the tid sits numerically BELOW the pid (post-wrap allocation). The deleting code was LIVE, not stale; "restart the old server" would have fixed nothing. **End-to-end reproduction on scratch copies of both trees, ephemeral ports, never :35110/:35113**: one real `POST /answer` with his payload against the pre-fix server took 63→50 answered, **13 entries destroyed**, file cut to exactly 200,366 chars — and returned **HTTP 202 success** while doing it; the fixed server, same request, 63→63 and +365 chars. **Fix in three commits**: `cdf611c0` adds `read_text_full` (a NAME, not a default — a leaked display default was the whole defect) and `rewrite_append_only`, ONE door doing whole-read → mutate → loss-check → atomic write, now used by `/ask` (`:4931`, a third destructive site), `/answer`, `/comment`, plus chat transcripts which had the same defect one route away; `1636b009` removes the CLASS by making `read_text` UNBOUNDED by default with `read_text_bounded` returning `(text, truncated)` as the only way to get a short read (reads limit+1 so an exactly-cap file is not miscalled cut), and makes `/reviewraw`+`/researchraw` REFUSE at their explicit 2,000,000 cap rather than serve half an HTML document that renders broken silently; `7da08cba` adds `qsnap.py`, an out-of-band snapshot net. **Guard judgement, checked not assumed**: I did NOT adopt "answered count must not decrease" — it fights folds. `first_lost_line` is a subsequence walk over NON-BLANK lines (blanks ignored because `append_human_question` legitimately collapses them). My first fold test asserted a fold passes it and **FAILED**: a fold moves entries across the section header so order changes and the guard refuses it, and no check is both strong enough to catch partial deletion and blind to reordering — so this is a SCOPED invariant, safe only because folds never traverse this door, and the test is inverted to pin that boundary and fail loudly if anyone ever wires a fold through it. On refusal nothing is written, `log_event` shouts onto the dashboard stream, and 500; refusing is safe ONLY because `log_submission` (#199) already has his words. **Collateral silent defect found and fixed**: the dashboard read questions.md, lessons.md (273,695) and handoffs.md (364,445) through the same capped default — roughly 45% of handoffs.md was invisible to the surface decisions were being made against, with nothing saying so; the questions.md read also feeds the signature store, so tail entries were losing their sigs. **qsnap armed out-of-process** (store `~/.cache/dreamwork/qsnap/`, nothing in `git status`, no restart of 2627314 or 3443196): inotify on the DIRECTORY (os.replace moves the inode) + 1s poll backstop, content-addressed gzip, keep 400/128MB pruning OLDEST first, and it DETECTS — answered/open counts per content, alert on a DROP only (a fold RAISES answered, and an alert that fires on every fold is one nobody reads). Its own test found a real bug: prune sorted by NAME, and names carry a one-SECOND stamp, so same-second snapshots sorted by sha at random — which is the burst case this net exists for; now sorted by mtime. **Red-proofed six ways, each producing the discriminating failure** (reverting the whole read reds the real-payload test with entries lost; neutering `first_lost_line` reds 5; reintroducing the `read_text(qpath` construct reds the source guard; restoring the 200_000 default reds 5; breaking limit+1 reds 3; name-sorted prune reds the prune test) — **and one GREEN red-run was caught rather than trusted**: the first end-to-end run showed the pre-fix server losing nothing, because the fixture came out at 161,792 chars, under the cap; enlarged and re-run before believing it. `test_watch.py`'s #370 atomicity guard broke legitimately (the write moved into the door) and was STRENGTHENED not relaxed — it now forbids the `read_text(qpath` construct by name; its two fileview proofs derived their oversize figure from `read_text.__defaults__[0]`, now None, and name the historical cap instead. Full pytest **1855 passed**, 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (`TestDocMapPlans::test_this_repo_maps_its_own_plans`, #630's unregistered component-transition doc-map row — confirmed pre-existing by re-running with my changes stashed; deliberately not touched, another lane owns that file; **recommend filing**). Restores were `cp`+`cmp`, never `git checkout` (#349); scratch copies only, the live questions.md never edited by me; `git commit --only`; no merge, push or `attn`.
